## Practice Question 1
Brilliant Education Holding Ltd (“BEHL”) was incorporated in Hong Kong in 2007. BEHL and its subsidiaries (the “Group”) were founded and wholly owned by Dr Albert Kam, a child psychologist. The Group operates under the trade name “Brilliant Kids”.

Recognising that children learn a second language the most between birth and five years of age, the Group has been engaging in developing and selling children’s English educational kits in Hong Kong. After a decade of growth, Brilliant Kids has become a common household name in Hong Kong.

The Group has a financial year end of 30 June, and plans to list on the Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited in the coming few years. The principal activities of BEHL and its subsidiaries include:
- Design and develop English learning materials, and license the use of the materials to fellow subsidiaries of the Group and third parties.
- Sell English educational kits to retail customers at its online store and store outlets in Causeway Bay, Kowloon Tong and Shatin.
The principal activity of BE Consulting is designing and developing English learning kits for use by fellow subsidiaries. BE Consulting self-develops and owns all the copyrights of the educational content developed.

“Sing along with Kocky” is a popular educational music video series developed by the Group in 2016, and the main character, Kocky, as well as his friends, would appear in the music videos encouraging children English learning through singing. The Group updates the existing content and also adds new content to “Sing along with Kocky” series continuously. In addition, some new characters (as Kocky’s friends) would be regularly added to the series, and the images of the characters would evolve over time.

**License arrangement with Sommana Group (Thailand) Limited**
BE Consulting has entered into a license agreement with Sommana Group (Thailand) Limited (“SGL”), a third party Thailand publisher for the content of the “Sing along with Kocky” series. SGL is involved in publishing English learning materials in Thailand. Besides, it also has around 30 learning centres in Bangkok delivering English learning courses which targets children and young adults in Thailand.

Under the License agreement, BE Consulting granted the rights for SGL to assess and publish all the latest content of “Sing along with Kocky” series exclusively in Thailand for a license period of three years from 1 January 2016 to 31 December 2018. There is no minimum guaranteed level of payment under the license agreement and the Group would receive a share of 3% of the proceeds in return. The term of the license agreement stated that either parties can terminate the contract by paying a penalty of four million Thai Baht (THB).
Plan to expand to Thailand

The Group considers expanding its business in Thailand and is now in the process of formulating the expansion strategy by either setting up a new company or through acquisition in Thailand.

As the Group has established a long-term relationship with SGL, Ms Kelly Tang, the Chief Financial Officer (“CFO”), suggested to expand through acquisition of 75% equity interest in SGL (the “Acquisition”). The Acquisition will be at the consideration of THB80 million settled in cash.

Required:
Assume that you are Ms Kelly Tang, the CFO of the Group. With respect to the proposed acquisition of Sommana Group (Thailand) Limited (“SGL”), prepare a memo to Dr Albert Kam, the CEO of the Group, advising the following:
- (a) advantages and disadvantages of the expansion strategy in Thailand through acquisition of SGL.
- (b) the importance of performing the due diligence on SGL, explaining the due diligence process, matters to consider in relation to the financial due diligence, as well as the necessity to extend the due diligence to non-financial areas.

## Q1 suggested answer
Date: XX / XX / XX
To: Dr Albert Kam, CEO
From: Kelly Tang, CFO
Subject: Proposed acquisition of Sommana Group (Thailand) Limited (“SGL”)
In respect to the proposed acquisition of SGL, please find below for (i) the advantages and disadvantages of the expansion strategy through acquisition; and (ii) the importance of performing due diligence.
(a) Advantages and disadvantages of the expansion strategy through acquisition
### Advantages:
#### **Access to overseas market**
Acquiring SGL is an easier way to enter the local market and expand its operation in an overseas market, as the Group is not very familiar with the Thailand market.
#### **Speed**
Acquisition of SGL that is already in operation in the overseas market is a quicker way to implement the expansion plan, as local Thai people are familiar with SGL.
#### **Lower cost**
It may be a cheaper way to acquire SGL with productive capacity in overseas market, as the labour cost is relatively low in Thailand.
#### **Acquisition of intangible assets**
The Group can also acquire intangible assets of SGL, such as, brand recognition, reputation, customer loyalty and intellectual properties that are difficult to achieve by the Group itself, as local Thai people are familiar with SGL local brand.

### Disadvantages:
#### Exposure to business and financial risk
SGL may not perform as good as expected that may have a significant impact to the Group.
#### High premium
The Group may pay a high premium over its market value and it may make the acquisition unprofitable.
#### Managerial competence
SGL may request to retain its own management; therefore, the Group may encounter difficulty in controlling SGL.
#### Integration problem
SGL encounters integration problems to fit in the culture and management style of the Group.

## (b) The importance of performing due diligence
The objective of the due diligence exercise is to determine whether the information about SGL is correct, accurate, and properly disclosed. Due diligence process in acquiring SGL is crucial, as it involves the appropriate amount in buying SGL at a right time.

Due diligence is the investigation process of a potential investment that is usually conducted by a professional firm. The due diligence process is usually conducted by a team consists of experts in different areas.

The process involves an examination of operations and management and verification of material facts; and the team will request documents from various departments in order to determine whether the information is correct.

In Hong Kong, the Securities and Futures Commission (“SFC”) requires the management of the listed company to conduct the necessary due diligence before the acquisition of the targeted firm. Since the Group plans for the listing, it is best practice for the Group to follow the SFC rules to perform the due diligence on SGL.

In relation to the financial due diligence, the team should address the following areas or matters, such as:
- Whether the assets are of good quality and possess any impairments.
- Whether the intangible assets (brands, reputation, goodwill etc.) are positive and can generate future economic benefits.
- Whether contingencies, long term liability commitments, and leases have been made.
- Whether the cash flow is positive.
Since the Statement of Financial Position reflects the current position of the assets and liabilities of SGL, the team should look at the items listed on The Statement of Financial Position; and forecast and identify other variables that may be at risk.

The team may not be able to detect any fraud, irregularities, omission of relevant information, and hidden error etc. due to collusion if only focus on the financial due diligence.

In order to provide a more comprehensive picture of SGL, other areas, such as, operation, legal, and corporate culture etc. are areas or matters that also draw the attention of the team.

#### Commercial due diligence
Considering the acquisition of SGL, the team should also conduct the commercial due diligence, such as SWOT analysis, to make sure that SGL is strategically linked with the Company as a whole, as the operation and production environment in Thailand are different in Hong Kong.
#### Legal due diligence
The team should also look at the legal documents, such as Sales and Purchase agreements of SGL, to make sure that there is no hidden contingent liability as the legal environment in Thailand and Hong Kong is different.
#### Corporate culture
The team should evaluate the management philosophy of SGL to make sure that the overall attitude of the management and the corporate culture of SGL are similar with the Company as Thai culture is different than Chinese culture.

## Practice question 2
In February 2020, DPA Bank Limited (“DPA Bank”) found it hard to pull off a profitable banking business in the Hong Kong market anymore using high cost brick and mortar operations due to the expensive space and labour costs in the city. Besides, the increasing popularity of internet finance among Hong Kong consumers is obvious. Hong Kong, according to the latest survey, has the third highest digital banking penetration in the Asia Pacific region, following South Korea and China. The survey has found that over 70% of the population uses online or mobile banking.
In this connection, DPA Bank plans to acquire HK Fintech Bank Limited (“HK Fintech Bank”) by exchanging 900 million of its shares for all the shares in HK Fintech Bank. DPA Bank was listed on the Hong Kong Stock Exchange in 2004 whereas HK Fintech Bank was listed on the Hong Kong Stock Exchange in 2014. Assume that the two banks are carrying no debt.
HK Fintech Bank is the sole online-only bank operated by the city’s dominant messaging app Whatsapp. The Hong Kong Monetary Authority is hoping that HK Fintech Bank will spur growth in the banking industry that has experienced slow growth amid increasing credit costs, tightening interest margins and heavy regulation. Dr Darren Yip, the CEO of HK Fintech Bank, established the firm in 2006 after graduating from Harvard University in the US. The employees of HK Fintech Bank are generally happy with the growth of the business. Staff turnover is lower than the industry average and morale is positive in general. Stock compensation is widely used to reward staff’s outstanding performance as a bonus payment. Darren is aware that there are other potential suitors interested in taking control of his bank from Hong Kong and China.
By acquiring HK Fintech Bank, Ms Sophia Chan, the CEO of DPA Bank announced during the press release in March 2020 of its intention to buy, “The marriage of the two banks will likely transform conventional financial services in Hong Kong”. It is generally believed by the banking industry that the new player in internet finance will lead the industry in some major changes in the years to come. Digital banking is the way to go due to the sustainable cost advantage gain at the end of the day. The traditional banks will have to accelerate branch closures. Yet there are also critics who question the sustainability of internet banks’ expansion.
The acquisition, after eight months of negotiation between the two banks, voting by the shareholders of the target, was finally approved with the regulator’s green light in November 2020. The merged bank kept the acquirer’s bank name after the merger.
Based on the latest consultant’s opinion, DPA Bank plans to implement a balanced scorecard. Its mission statement highlights that the bank builds top quality and reliable services at competitive prices. DPA Bank will further expand its banking service from traditional face to face to online banking. It will strive to deliver its banking services through an internet platform and mobile platforms to reach customers anywhere at anytime.
Required:
(a) Based on PESTEL analysis, describe the most significant factor that supports Sophia’s strategic move in this merger.
(b) Analyse the three leading reasons to explain the high premium offered by DPA Bank.
(c) Explain three target firms’ characteristics leading to a high premium being required to be paid to HK Fintech Bank.
(d) Advise as to performance objective for each of the four perspectives of the balanced scorecard of DPA Bank. Recommend one measure for each performance objective.

## Suggested answer
(a) There is likely to be technological change in banking services. With the speed of computers getting faster and the popularity of mobile technology, banking services could be conducted easily online and on mobile platforms. Customers find it more and more convenient to bank using these platforms. Financial institutions could continue to improve handling information in sales and finance.
As a result, this merger could enhance a new organisation structure to exploit this new technology. Moreover, it serves as a new media for communication with customers and within business to facilitate banking business globally.

(b) Typically, a target premium is paid to the target’s shareholders. The two leading explanations include pre-emptive bidding and target resistance. The pre-emptive bidding reason suggests that takeover premiums are determined not only by actual, but also by potential competition. If entry into takeover contests is costly, as an initial bidder, DPA Bank could deter a rival by making a bid that signals a high valuation for the target. Premiums paid in single bidder takeovers then reflect the cost of deterring rival bidders from entry.
According to the target resistance explanation, target shareholders could resist takeover proposals if the premium offered is not high enough. This resistance could reflect information on future takeover opportunities or the value of large shareholders’ private benefits. High premiums in successful single bidder contests are mainly determined by target resistance.
The accelerated time to market is another factor because buying a business is faster than building a business. There would be synergy between an on-line and off-line bank.
Besides, DPA Bank could leapfrog the learning curve, i.e. avoid the business risk of exploring the fintech market from scratch.

(c) A target with an above median entrenchment situation and governance issues exhibits higher resistance. The presence of a founder-CEO is also associated with higher target resistance, consistent with the notion that founders attach a high value to maintaining control of the target Bank. Besides, a relative low managerial percentage ownership in the target firm is correlated with higher resistance. This result lends support to the view that stock-based compensation aligns the interests of managers and shareholders and therefore it will likely reduce target firm managerial resistance to takeover offers.
Resistance is more severe for a target that is subsequently acquired in a separate contest. This is consistent with the idea that a target that is more likely to anticipate an attractive future takeover opportunity exhibits higher resistance to the current offer.

(d)
#### Customer perspective:
The customer perspective analyses the role of customers in DPA Bank’s success. Performance objective is to increase the size of loyal customer numbers.
Measurement of number of return customers and number of new customers.
#### Financial perspective:
The financial perspective analyses the financial results of DPA Bank’s operation. Performance objective is to achieve the profit level in the top three quintile in the banking industry.
Measurement of operating profit margin and absolute profit level.
#### Internal business process perspective:
The internal business process perspective analyses DPA Bank’s internal procedures and practices leading to its success. Performance objective is to continue to innovate a quality service to customers.
Measurement of repeating customer frequency, referral customer frequency, customer satisfaction level quality control, compliance of banking internal process efficiency, e.g. customer order handling speed, the frequency and handling of customer complaints, innovation, productivity.
#### Learning and growth perspective:
The learning and growth perspective analyses the continuous improvement effort in DPA Bank’s success. Performance objective is investing in productive staff and promoting life-long learning among employees.
Measurement of employee turnover rate and retention rate, number of suggestions for improvement from staff. The Bank could provide the right incentive to motivate employee’s performance.


[^1]:	1 Octopus, launched in 1997, is a predominant contactless stored value smart card for travelling and making electronic payments in online or offline systems in Hong Kong.
